#+title: Investigation: ORE Studio failed builds by day
#+description: Every failed CI run of ORE Studio from 2026-09-19 to 2026-11-18, day by day, with its failing job, step, reason, pattern and whether compass could catch it before a build.
#+type: investigation
#+level: s1
#+filetags: :gates:llm:catch_llm_error_patterns:sprint_01:v0:
#+created: 2026-11-18
#+updated: 2026-11-18

A point-in-time record, written while the story that commissioned it was open, of an [[id:A7F8D9E2-3C4B-4A5D-B67C-8E9F0A1B2C3D][investigation]] into why ORE Studio's CI builds failed, day by day, and which failures compass could have caught before the build. It is evidence for the [[id:694CC015-2319-4A60-A912-C47D01BA70E1][Mine failed builds]] task and was written on 2026-11-18.

It is a snapshot, not a description of the system. The patterns, their
ranking and the candidates for new checks are in
[[id:6947B0D8-A1AE-4180-B2D9-D33FF02C1A3B][Investigation: ORE Studio failed build patterns]], which also says how the
data was fetched.

* Context

The [[id:63D5482E-E1E7-45B0-883F-3B2DDFA2B447][Catch the errors LLMs introduce]] story needs to know which errors
reach ORE Studio's CI and which of them a check could stop before a build.
This page is the raw record: every failed run, with the reason it failed.

* Methodology

=build/scripts/mine_failed_builds.py report= writes the block below from
the cache that =mine_failed_builds.py fetch= filled. The summary page gives
the commands, the date range and the counts.

- A row is one workflow, event, step, reason and pattern on one day, and
  links every run that failed that way. A build matrix whose jobs fail
  alike, or a pull request pushed several times, shares a row; the Job
  column names the first job and counts the others.
- A run whose jobs failed in different ways has a row for each way.
- Step is the first step of the job that failed. Reason is the first real
  error of that step, normalised: paths, line numbers, hashes, numbers,
  UUIDs and timestamps are replaced by =<path>=, =N= and the like, and the
  text is cut at 140 characters.
- Verdict is one of: /already caught/ (a compass codec catches it today),
  /could be caught/ (a check could, reading data compass loads), /not
  catchable/ (only a build, a test run or the runner shows it) and
  /unclassified/. "(guess)" marks a verdict inferred from the message
  alone. The summary page names the codec, the data or the reason for each
  pattern.
- Failed-job minutes add up the wall time of the failed jobs. They leave
  out the jobs of the same run that passed or were cancelled.

* Paths taken

The day of a run is the UTC day GitHub created it. A run that a later
attempt passed is not listed, because GitHub reports the conclusion of the
latest attempt.

* Conclusions

# BEGIN generated failures_by_day (build/scripts/mine_failed_builds.py)
